AI 資本支出侵蝕現金流，科技巨頭財報拖累科技股 
                                   2026 年 10 月 30 日 
一、 市場動態 
1. AI 資本支出與現金流疑慮再起，那斯達克領跌：美股 10 月 29 日主要指數收黑，S&P 
500 下跌 0.30%、Nasdaq 下跌 1.38%、道瓊下跌 0.25%、費半下跌 1.42%，費半 ETF
盤後跌幅進一步擴大至逾 3%。市場下跌主要因 Alphabet 財報營收與獲利低於預期、
搜尋廣告業務成長放緩，加上大幅增加資本支出導致單季自由現金流轉負，股價重挫
12.64%，再度引發市場對 AI 投資報酬的疑慮，賣壓亦擴及半導體及軟體類股。其他
盤後公布財報的科技公司表現亦出現雜音，Amazon 資本支出明顯高於預期，近 12
個月自由現金流轉為淨流出，Intel 則因 PC 需求疲弱下修財測，使科技股盤後賣壓進
一步擴大。不過，當日 S&P 500 約 81%成分股上漲，顯示市場賣壓主要集中於 AI 相
關大型科技股，而非整體市場同步走弱。 
2. Fed 按兵不動但兩票主張升息，12 月升息機率仍高：Fed 於 10 月 28 日以 10 比 2 維
持利率於 3.75%～4.00%，Cook 與 Hammack 主張升息 1 碼，期貨市場隱含 12 月
升息機率約 85%。9 月 PCE 年增 3.6%，10 年期美債殖利率在 5%附近震盪。 
3. 中東情勢緊張，油價維持百元以上高檔：布蘭特原油 10 月 28 日升至每桶 115 美元附
近，WTI 亦站上 102 美元。高油價增加通膨與 Fed 升息壓力，也推升 AI 資料中心電
力成本，持續壓抑科技股評價。

二、 大型科技企業財報重點： 
1. 雲端業務全面加速，AI 需求仍未降溫：本週公布財報的大型科技企業中，雲端業務成
長普遍加速。Alphabet 旗下 Google Cloud 營收 307.09 億美元、年增 103%，TPU
系統銷售成為重要動能；Microsoft Azure 年增 48%，下季財測進一步加速至 49～
50%；Amazon AWS 營收 459.8 億美元、年增 39.3%，為近五年最快增速。Intel 資
料中心與 AI 營收亦年增 78%，創 15 年來最強成長。各家均表示算力需求仍高於可用
供給，顯示 AI 基礎建設需求尚未出現降溫跡象。 
2. 資本支出持續攀升，自由現金流表現決定股價分歧：Microsoft 於 10 月 28 日盤後公
布 2027 會計年度第一季財報，營收 922.79 億美元、年增 18.8%，營業利益率達
49.8%，自由現金流亦維持正值，整體財報優於市場預期。相較之下，Alphabet 雲端
業務表現亮眼，但廣告業務成長未達預期，且單季資本支出達 513.55 億美元，導致
自由現金流轉為負 90.65 億美元，股價於 10 月 29 日重挫 12.64%；Amazon 單季資
本支出則達 773 億美元，近 12 個月自由現金流轉為淨流出 424 億美元，加上第四季
營收財測中值低於預期，盤後股價下跌逾 6%。資本支出指引方面，Alphabet 維持 2026
年 1,950 億至 2,050 億美元，Amazon 維持約 2,200 億美元，Microsoft 則因第三方

產能交付延遲，微幅下修至約 1,700 億美元，三家公司均預期 2027 年資本支出將進
一步增加。（詳見圖 1）。

3. PC 需求疲弱拖累 Intel 財測，Oracle 明確籌資路徑緩解融資疑慮：Intel 第三季營收
165 億美元、年增 21%，non-GAAP EPS 0.52 美元優於預期，但記憶體等零組件成
本上升壓抑 PC 需求，客戶端運算營收年減 5%，第四季營收財測 150～160 億美元低
於市場預期約 171 億美元，盤後股價重挫逾 20%。另一方面，Oracle 於 10 月 28 日
投資人日設定 FY2030 營收目標逾 2,250 億美元，並承諾 FY2030 前額外外部籌資上

限為 200 億美元（股、債各 100 億美元）、自由現金流於 FY2028 下半年轉正，股價
當日大漲約 11%，有助降低市場對 AI 基礎建設融資風險的擔憂（詳見圖 2）。

三、 市場看法：
1. 市場不再單純獎勵 AI 投資，開始要求可驗證的財務報酬：Microsoft 與 Alphabet 同

樣大舉投資 AI，股價反應卻明顯分歧，關鍵在於 AI 投資是否已轉化為獲利與現金流。
我們認為，市場評價標準已由「是否投資 AI」轉向「AI 投資能否帶來足夠的營收成長
與現金流」，具備穩定現金流與明確商業化能力的企業將相對受青睞，而自由現金流持
續為負、高度依賴外部融資的業者則可能面臨評價壓縮。

2. 雲端未認列合約持續擴大，AI 需求尚未出現結構性反轉：Microsoft 商業 RPO 達 6,770
億美元、年增 73%，Oracle RPO 約 6,640 億美元，AWS 與 Google Cloud backlog
亦分別達 5,780 億及 5,610 億美元，四家合計約 2.48 兆美元（詳見圖 3）。這顯示目
前市場疑慮主要在於投資回收期與資金壓力，而非終端需求惡化；只要雲端業者持續
擴充算力，先進製程、伺服器與資料中心供應鏈中期仍具支撐。
3. 資本支出 2027 年仍將成長，台灣 AI 供應鏈持續受惠：四大雲端／平台業者 2026 年

資本支出合計約 7,200～7,400 億美元，且 2027 年仍將增加。元大投顧認為，Google
擴大 TPU 對外銷售及 Microsoft 持續部署 GPU 與自研 Maia 加速器，將帶動伺服器
ODM、散熱及機殼需求，受惠供應鏈包括廣達、奇鋐、雙鴻、健策、富世達及聯發科。
融資面則宜留意 AI 相關企業公司債利差及再融資條件變化。
4. Fed 鷹派立場限制評價空間，短線震盪可能延續：12 月升息預期升溫、通膨與油價維
持高檔，使 10 年期美債殖利率停留在 5%附近，壓抑高評價科技股評價擴張。只要
AI 需求未出現結構性反轉，維持短線審慎，對科技股中期偏正向看法。
圖 3：主要雲端業者未認列合約（Backlog／RPO）規模（單位：億美元）
公司 未認列合約規模 相關說明
Microsoft 6,770 商業 RPO 年增 73%，約 30%將於未來 12 個月認列
Oracle 約 6,640 RPO 年增約 2,090 億美元
Amazon（AWS） 5,780 backlog 年增率達三位數

Alphabet（Google 雲） 5,610 略高於 55%預計於未來 24 個月認列
合計 約 24,800 約 2.48 兆美元
註：Microsoft 與 Alphabet 為 2026 年 9 月季底數據；Oracle 為最新公布之 RPO。資料來源：各公司財報，元大銀行整理
四、市場展望：
1. 科技股短線波動較大，中期看好現金流穩健的 AI 受惠企業：本週財報顯示雲端營收加速
成長與未認列合約持續擴大，AI 需求仍強；但資本支出攀升、自由現金流轉弱，加上 Fed
可能於 12 月升息，短線科技股波動恐將延續。中期而言，AI 產業將由全面成長轉向基本
面分化，具技術優勢、財務結構穩健且 AI 投資已能轉化為獲利的企業，如雲端與 AI 軟體
平台領先者，以及先進製程、伺服器、散熱等關鍵供應鏈，風險報酬相對有利；對高度依
賴外部融資、自由現金流持續為負的企業則宜維持審慎，並留意零組件成本上升對 PC 等
消費性電子需求的排擠效應。
2. 美債避險功能仍在，中天期債券風險報酬相對均衡：10 月 29 日科技股下跌時，10 年期

美債殖利率由約 5.05%回落至約 4.99%，顯示美債仍具一定避險效果。惟 Fed 鷹派立場
及油價高檔使短天期公債易受升息預期影響，超長天期公債則面臨財政赤字與期限溢價壓
力，中天期公債風險報酬相對均衡。投資級債中期維持偏正向；高收益債則須留意 AI 相
關發行人自由現金流轉負及利差擴大風險。

3. 下週財報焦點：Apple、Palantir 與 AMD 接棒，檢驗 AI 需求延續性：下週（11/2～11/6）
大型科技財報持續登場。Apple 及 Palantir 將於 11 月 2 日（美東時間）盤後公布，市場
將關注 iPhone 新機需求、記憶體成本對毛利率的影響，以及 AI 軟體商業化進度；AMD
與 Arista Networks 將於 11 月 3 日盤後公布，AMD 將提供 AI 加速器（Instinct GPU）
與伺服器 CPU 需求的最新證據，Arista 則可觀察資料中心網路設備需求。在本週雲端業

者確認資本支出持續成長後，下週財報將進一步檢驗 AI 供應鏈訂單能否轉化為營收與獲
利（詳見圖 4）。

上週重要市場資訊
 美國期中選舉由民主黨贏得參議院及眾議院多數席次，川普剩餘兩年任內將面
臨分立政府的局面，市場擔憂民主黨未來傾向加強 AI監管，恐削弱美國經濟成
長動能。
 歐元區 10月 CPI年增率自前月的 3.8%上升至 4%，創下三年來的新高，通膨面
臨上行風險。歐洲央行管委暨斯洛伐克央行總裁卡日米爾(Peter Kazimir)表示，
歐洲央行將持續升息，以防止高通膨根深蒂固；德國央行總裁納格爾(Joachim
Nagel)亦表示通膨前景尚未改善，歐洲央行應於 12 月升息。多位歐洲央行官員
發表偏鷹言論，市場預期歐洲央行未來一年內將再升息二至三次，升息預期升
溫，預計將支撐歐元走勢。
 美國 10 月 ISM 製造業 PMI 為 55.5，高於市場預期，製造業擴張速度加快；另
10 月 ISM 非製造業 PMI為 54.5，持穩於景氣榮枯線之上，顯示美國整體基本面
佳，預計將為美元提供支撐。

 美國 10 月非農就業人數增加 16.5 萬人，優於市場預期的 6.5 萬人，另 10 月失
業率亦由前月的 4.2%降至 4.1%，顯示勞動市場仍具韌性，在美國經濟穩健及通
膨維持高位的情況下，強化聯準會 12月升息預期。
 澳洲央行於 11/3 將現金基準利率調升一碼至 4.85%，係今年以來第五次升息並
創下 2008 年 11 月以來新高水準。然而，此次九名委員中有三人主張維持利率
不變，投票結果出現分歧，加上貨幣政策委員會聲明中，刪除有關可能進一步升
息的明確表述，令市場預期澳洲央行將於 12月暫停升息。

3  兆豐國際商業銀行 http://www.megabank.com.tw/ 
 美元兌新台幣匯率走勢  
Nov/09/2026

＜上週走勢＞ 
    上週台幣匯價高點為 32.500，低點在 33.040，以 32.768 作收，較前一週收盤價
貶值 2 角 9 分。 
    上週台幣走貶，週初，台股開低，市場對 AI投資支出及科技股估值的疑慮，致
使外資連續多日賣超台股並匯出，台幣連貶數日，加以週間美國期中選舉結果出爐，
民主黨勝選，市場擔憂 AI產業監管趨嚴，台股重挫，外資上週累計賣超台股 5,244
億，外資強勢匯出之下，儘管逢高有出口商拋匯抑制台幣貶值速度，台幣仍貶破 33
整數大關，一度觸及年內低點 33.040，惟週五午盤過後，外資及壽險轉向匯入，加
以出口商拋匯需求，台幣跌幅收斂，終場在央行調節之下收在 32.768。

＜未來分析＞ 
     預估本週台幣走勢偏弱。就國際市場而言，美國非農就業數據意外高於預期、
通膨壓力仍在，強化市場對於聯準會 12月升息預期，高利率將有助國際美元走升；
就台灣市場來看，市場對 AI產業資本支出、融資成本及信用風險產生疑慮，加以民
主黨勝選，引發市場對 AI監管政策收緊與資料中心建設放緩的擔憂，外資持續賣超
台股，令台幣持續面臨貶值的壓力，惟出口商拋匯需求待消化，將抑制台幣貶值速
度，綜合以上，預估本週台幣走勢偏弱。

＜預估本週＞ 美元兌台幣：32.600-33.200

美元兌人民幣匯率走勢  
Nov/09/2026

＜上週走勢＞ 
上週人民幣先升後貶。週初，國際美元偏弱震盪，人民幣受益走升，境內人民
幣相隔四年多首次升破 6.65整數大關；惟週二中國央行宣布調升外匯存款準備金率，
顯示其抑制單邊升值預期的意圖，人民幣大幅走貶；週間，市場持續消化人行政策
影響，人民幣走貶，最低一度貶至 6.6855，不過美國期中選舉結果出爐，市場不確
定性下降，國際油價、美債殖利率及美元指數皆下跌，緩解人民幣的賣壓，惟中國
國有大行逢低持續買進美元，人民幣反彈力道有限，終場境外人民幣收在 6.6736，
週線貶值 212 點，境內人民幣則收在 6.6763，週線貶值 203 點。

＜未來分析＞ 
     預估本週人民幣區間震盪。就國際市場來看，美國非農就業人數新增 16.5 萬
人，就業率下降至 4.1%，勞動力市場意外強勁，強化市場對於聯準會 12 月再次升
息的預期，利差因素支撐美元指數反彈，將抑制人民幣走升；就中國國內市場來看，
人行最新政策顯示其抑制人民幣單邊升值的意圖，惟第四季為結匯旺季，出口商實
質拋匯需求部分緩解人民幣貶值壓力；最後就技術面來看，USD/CNH 日線圖中，
USD/CNH重返五日均線之上，顯示人民幣強勢升值的趨勢暫歇，綜合以上，預估本
週人民幣區間震盪。

＜預估本週＞ 美元兌人民幣：6.6600-6.6900

美元兌日圓匯率走勢  
         Nov/09/2026

＜上週走勢＞ 
上週日圓走弱。週初，市場等待美國期中選舉結果，交投偏謹慎觀望，USD/JPY
在 158 附近波動；週間，美國期中選舉由民主黨取得參眾議院多數席次，政治僵局
擔憂令美元走弱，USD/JPY最低來到 157.39；臨近週末，美國公布就業數據優於市
場預期，USD/JPY止跌回升，終場收在 158.45，週線上漲 100 點。 
＜未來分析＞ 
  預估本週 USD/JPY區間震盪，主要原因如下：一、日本央行於季度展望報告中
表示，日本近期實際利率上升，基礎通膨率已加速升至 2%附近，加上日本央行近期
亦強調政策已進入避免通膨超標為重點的新階段，進一步強化了其鷹派立場，市場
預期日本最快將於 12月再次升息，預計將支撐日圓走升。二、美國 10月 ISM製造
業 PMI 為 55.5，優於市場預期，顯示製造業擴張速度加快；另外，美國公布 10 月
非農就業人數增加 16.5 萬人，優於市場預期的 6.5 萬人，美國經濟強勁、勞動市場
仍具韌性，強化聯準會 12月升息預期，美元持穩，預計將有利 USD/JPY走勢。三、
日本財務大臣片山皋月重申美日聯合匯率聲明仍具效力，將於必要時採取外匯干預
行動，以維護外匯市場有序運作，將限縮 USD/JPY上行空間。四、就技術面而言，
在日線圖中，USD/JPY短中天期均線趨平，近期大致在 157 至 158.50 間盤整，匯價
暫無明確方向性。綜合以上，預估 USD/JPY本週呈現區間震盪的可能性較高。

＜預估本週＞ 美元兌日幣：157.00-159.50 
前週交易區間

美元兌歐元匯率走勢  
             Nov/09/2026

＜上週走勢＞ 
  上週 EUR/USD 先上後下。週初，在美伊傳出有望和談消息後，歐元自低點小
幅走升至 1.1237；週間，美國民主黨於期中選舉大獲全勝，川普選舉失利，美元偏
弱震盪；臨近週末，美國公布非農就業人數高於市場預期，歐元自高點回落，終場
收在 1.1143，週線下跌 12點。 
＜未來分析＞ 
  預估本週 EUR/USD震盪偏弱，主要原因如下：一、歐元區 10月 CPI年增率自
前月的 3.8%上升至 4%，創下三年來的新高，通膨面臨上行風險。此外，歐洲央行
管委暨斯洛伐克央行總裁卡日米爾(Peter Kazimir)表示，歐洲央行將持續升息，以防
止高通膨根深蒂固，並表示目前主要考量已非是否需採取進一步行動，而是須衡量
應升息幾次方能將通膨拉回 2%目標水準，市場預期歐洲央行未來一年內將再升息
二至三次，升息預期升溫，預計將支撐歐元走勢。二、美國公布 10月非農就業人數
增加 16.5 萬人，優於市場預期的 6.5 萬人，另 10 月失業率亦由前月的 4.2%降至
4.1%，顯示勞動市場仍具韌性，強化了聯準會 12月升息預期，美元再度走升至 102，
預計將限縮歐元走升空間。三、技術面而言，在日線圖中，EUR/USD各天期均線向
下發散，近期匯價多次向上測試 1.1270 均拉回留下長上影線，顯示上方仍有賣壓待
消化。綜合以上，預估 EUR/USD本週呈現震盪偏弱的可能性較高。

＜預估本週＞ 歐元兌美元：1.1030-1.1250

                           新台幣利率走勢分析

115.11.02-115.11.06 
銀行隔夜拆款利率 0.805% ~ 0.825%  
30天期短票利率   1.67% ~ 1.69% 
公債－10 年期-A15109R 1.885% 
－ 5年期- A15108 1.725%

一、 台幣貨幣市場部分，受台股重挫影響，令週五資金需求上揚，但提存期初市場
資金充裕，短率持穩於近期區間。 
週二央行標售 364天期定存單 1100億元，加權平均得標利率為 1.577%，低於
市場預期區間 1.59%-1.61%，較上次標售結果下跌 2.5個基本點。 
週五隔夜拆款利率區間在 0.805%~0.825%， 30 天期短票主要成交在
1.67%~1.69%，利率較上週上揚 1 個基本點。 
二、 本週市場交投維持冷清，10 年期指標券 A15109R及 5年期指標券 A15108均
未成交，週五等殖系統參考報價中價分別為 1.885%、1.725%。 
股市承壓帶動避險需求，美債利率下滑，令先前台債偏空氛圍緩解，週三標售
30年期公債得標利率 2.267%，低於市場預期區間 2.27%~2.30%，投標倍數 1.85
倍，較上次 1.55 倍提升，不過考量台灣年底仍有升息可能，將抑制買盤向下
追價意願，預估台債利率區間盤整，預估 5 年券區間為 1.70%~1.75%，10 年
券區間為 1.85%~1.90%。

### Page 1

ab 19 November 2026
Global Research
Gudeng Precision Industrial
Broad-based 3Q beat sets up a solid 2027
Solid Q3 earnings beat; momentum continuing into Q4 and 2027
Gudeng’s Q326 results came in above consensus, with revenue 5% and EPS 16% above
VA consensus. The beat was broad-based, driven by stronger revenue, gross margin, and
opex leverage. We expect momentum to continue into Q426, with full-year 2026
revenue growing 34%, vs our prior estimate of 29%, driven by continued strength in

EUV and China FOUP. Q4 gross margin should sustain at 46% level, with better margin
of EUV mask pod. Looking into 2027, we estimate revenue growth of 31%, primarily
volume-driven, with upside if Gudeng can add more capacity for FOUP. Of note,
advanced packaging revenue could potentially reach NT$0.85bn, up meaningfully from
~NT$0.3bn in 2026. Below the line, we estimate FY27 gross margin of 46%, with
favorable mix from EUV and China FOUP, while the opex ratio should improve to 24%

from 25% in 2026.
EUV mask pod sales to grow 30% in 2027
EUV demand has remained consistently strong throughout 2026 and the sales should
grow 43% this year and 30% in 2027. Key drivers are the meaningful leading edge
expansion at TSMC, and Gudeng’s share gain in Intel. We estimate Gudeng’s EUV mask
pod shipment to Intel to likely close to triple YoY this year. DUV mask pod sales should
also grow 15-20% in 2027, supported by stable demand from China logic customers.

Meaningful recovery in China FOUP lifting ASP and gross margin
On top of the EUV strength, the business recovery in China FOUP is equally important in
re-accelerating Gudeng’s earnings and margin improvement. Gudeng has seen strong
demand from more diversified client base across logic and memory YTD, versus in the
past when growth was more reliant on tier 1 logic clients like SMIC. Considering the
tight supply at global peers, we anticipate the growth of China FOUP to sustain and

support 40% foup revenue growth in 2027. On TSMC side, we believe Gudeng should
also be improving product mix through 2027 with the upgraded diffuser wafer foup
with higher ASP and margin for N2. On advanced packaging, Gudeng is set to ramp up
sales more meaningfully from NT$300mn this year to NT$0.85bn in 2027, mostly
through large panel FOUP for substrate makers, but also good volume for CoWoS
related FOUP.
Valuation: Maintain Buy with lower price target of NT$610 from NT$710

Post Q3 results, we upgraded 2026/27 EPS by 14% and 2%. We lower our price target
from NT$710 to NT$610 based on a lower multiple of 26x 27-28E PE from 30x
previously, considering the broader sector de-rating and lower LT earnings CAGR of
26% from 29%. Maintain Buy.

Company Description
Gudeng Precision Industrial was founded in 1998 and entered semiconductor front-end
process supply chain in 2000. It manufactures reticle and wafer carriers,which provide
protection, delivery and storage solutions; it also supplies related equipment. According to

the company, it is the largest supplier of reticle carriers with a 70% market share globally and
holds an 85% market share for EUV POD, while its FOUP is adopted by over 50% of
customers in Greater China as the baseline.
Valuation Method and Risk Statement
We value Gudeng based on PE methodology.
Downside risk for the semis industry includes: 1) slower-than-expected N2 migration, 2)
geopolitical uncertainties that weigh on the semis cycle, and 3) meaningful deceleration of

new technology deveopment like Cloud AI, autonomous driving etc.
Downside risk for the company itself includes: 1) slower-than-expected share expansion in
FOUP market, 2) executions on pushing new products like diffuser FOUP, high NA EUV pods.

Investing in structured investments involves significant risks. For a detailed discussion of the risks involved in investing in any particular structured investment, you must read

the relevant offering materials for that investment. Structured investments are unsecured obligations of a particular issuer with returns linked to the performance of an
underlying asset. Depending on the terms of the investment, investors could lose all or a substantial portion of their investment based on the performance of the underlying
asset. Investors could also lose their entire investment if the issuer becomes insolvent. UBS does not guarantee in any way the obligations or the financial condition of any

issuer or the accuracy of any financial information provided by any issuer. Structured investments are not traditional investments and investing in a structured investment is
not equivalent to investing directly in the underlying asset. Structured investments may have limited or no liquidity, and investors should be prepared to hold their investment
to maturity. The return of structured investments may be limited by a maximum gain, participation rate or other feature. Structured investments may include call features and,

if a structured investment is called early, investors would not earn any further return and may not be able to reinvest in similar investments with similar terms. Structured
investments include costs and fees which are generally embedded in the price of the investment. The tax treatment of a structured investment may be complex and may differ
from a direct investment in the underlying asset. UBS and its employees do not provide tax advice. Investors should consult their own tax advisor about their own tax situation

before investing in any securities.

applies and OSATs clients have thus shown strong interest in adopting Hon's mega tray handler design when

### Page 3

Hon Precision (7769.TW)
25 November 2026 Citi Research
3
Hon Precision
Valuation
Our target price of NT$10,000 is based on a target PER multiple of 31x applied to the average of our 2027E/28E EPS estimates.
Our PER target of 31x is set at 0.5-std above its 1-year PER average, justified by its high share of the AI/HPC testing handler
market, which we estimate could support an earnings CAGR of >75% in 2025-28, higher than its 3-year CAGR at c40% in

2022-25. Our estimated >75% earning CAGR is also higher than the +40% average (Bloomberg consensus) of its global testing
equipment peers. As its peers have been trading at  average  35x PER on 2027/28 consensus EPS in the past 1 year, we believe
Hon Precision deserves a further re-rating to our target PER.
Risks
Our quantitative risk rating system, which looks at historical share price volatility, rates Hon Precision High Risk. However, we
are not assigning a High Risk rating as we forecast an earnings CAGR of >75%, significantly stronger than the growth outlook

for its global peers. Also, we note its dominant position in AI/HPC handler market and expanding market TAM opportunity,
which support our constructive view over its growth outlook.
Key downside risks that could impede the shares from reaching our target price: 1) Global AI demand slowdown  driving weaker
capacity build for semiconductor equipment; 2) unexpected share losses at its major AI projects; 3) slower-than-expected
capacity ramp at its new facilities, or the company taking a long time to find/secure new sites; 4) slower-than-expected

development of its CPO business; 5) shorter-than-expected testing time leading to lower demand for its products; 6) weak
adoption of high-premium cold plate products; and 7) increasing competition from new entrants.

Guide to Citi Research Fundamental Research Investment Ratings:
Citi Research stock recommendations include an investment rating and an optional risk rating to highlight high risk stocks.

Risk rating takes into account both price volatility and fundamental criteria. Stocks will either have no risk rating or a High risk
rating assigned.
Investment Ratings: Citi Research investment ratings are Buy, Neutral and Sell. Our ratings are a function of analyst
expectations of expected total return ("ETR") and risk. ETR is the sum of the forecast price appreciation (or depreciation) plus
the dividend yield for a stock within the next 12 months.  The target price is based on a 12 month time horizon. The Investment

rating definitions are: Buy (1) ETR of 15% or more or 25% or more for High risk stocks; and Sell (3) for negative ETR. Any covered
stock not assigned a Buy or a Sell is a Neutral (2). For stocks rated Neutral (2), if an analyst believes that there are insufficient
valuation drivers and/or investment catalysts to derive a positive or negative investment view, they may elect with the approval
of Citi Research management not to assign a target price and, thus, not derive an ETR. Citi Research may suspend its rating

and target price and assign “Rating Suspended” status for regulatory and/or internal policy reasons. Citi Research may also
suspend its rating and target price and assign "Under Review" status for other exceptional circumstances (e.g. lack of
information critical to the analyst's thesis, trading suspension) affecting the company and/or trading in the company's
securities. In both such situations, the rating and target price will show as “—“ and “-“ respectively in the rating history price

chart. Prior to 11 April 2022 Citi Research assigned “Under Review” status to both situations and prior to 11 Nov 2020 only in
exceptional circumstances. As soon as practically possible, the analyst will publish a note re-establishing a rating and
investment thesis. Investment ratings are determined by the ranges described above at the time of initiation of coverage, a
change in investment and/or risk rating, or a change in target price (subject to limited management discretion). At times, the

expected total returns may fall outside of these ranges because of market price movements and/or other short-term volatility
or trading patterns. Such interim deviations will be permitted but will become subject to review by Research Management.
Your decision to buy or sell a security should be based upon your personal investment objectives and should be made only
after evaluating the stock's expected performance and risk.
Catalyst Watch/Short Term Views (“STV”) Ratings Disclosure:

Catalyst Watch and STV Upside/Downside calls: Citi Research may also include a Catalyst Watch or STV Upside or Downside
call to indicate the analyst expects the share price to rise (fall) in absolute terms over a specified period of 30 or 90 days in
reaction to one or more specific near-term catalysts or events impacting the company or the market. A Catalyst Watch will be
published when Analyst confidence is high that an impact to share price will occur; it will be a STV when confidence level is

moderate. A Catalyst Watch or STV Upside/Downside call will automatically expire at the end of the specified 30/90 day
period. The Catalyst Watch will also be automatically removed if share price performance (calculated at market close) exceeds
15% against the direction of the call (unless over-ridden by the analyst). The analyst may also remove a Catalyst Watch or STV
call prior to the end of the specified period in a published research note. A Catalyst Watch/STV Upside or Downside call may be

our ratings distribution table for our Catalyst Watch/STV Upside/Downside rating system. However, we reiterate that we do

3Q26 Review: 4 분기 실적 반등 기대감 Up  
 투자의견 BUY(유지) 
 목표주가 600,000 원(유지) 
 현재주가 292,500 원(10/29)

 동사의 3분기 실적은 매출액 45.1조원(-3.4%yoy), 영업이익 2.68조원
(+5.5%yoy, OPM 5.9%), 지배이익 1.7조원(-24.6%yoy)을 기록하며 시장 
컨센서스를 상회. 도매 판매 감소, 원가 부담 증가 영향 있었으나 판매보증
충당금 축소와 원가 절감 활동을 통해 수익성 방어 
 실적 증감 요인: 영업이익 증감 요인은 물량 -1.11조원, 믹스/인센티브 -
0.57조원, 금융 -0.11조원, 환율 +1.28조원, 기타 +0.64조원 등. 원자재 
가격 상승으로 5천억원의 비용 증가 요인 있었으나 위기 대응 원가 절감 
활동으로 대부분 상쇄할 수 있었음. 전분기 대비 기말환율 급락함에 따라 
판매보증충당금 지난 해 1.26조원에서 340억원으로 약 1.23조원 감소. 판
보충 효과는 일회성에 그치겠지만 원가 절감 효과 컸던 점 긍정적  
 금융 부문: 금융부문 매출액은 전년비 2.8% 증가했지만 영업이익은 26% 
급감. 장기 금리 상승에 따른 이자 비용 증가와 기업금융 충당금 적립, 중고
차 관련 비용 확대가 수익성 악화의 주요 원인. 중고차 관련 비용은 EV 리
스 반납 차량의 매각 손실 확대에 기인한 것 
 4분기 전망: 동사는 국내 공장 정상화와 아반떼, 투싼, GV90, GV80 HEV 
등 주요 신차 효과를 통해 4분기 생산 확대와 주요 수출 시장 공급 회복이 
가능할 것으로 전망하였음. 원가 부담은 지속되나 물량 감소에 따른 영향에
서는 벗어날 수 있을 것. 원자재 가격 상승에 대응해 원가절감 프로그램 확
대와 신차 출시를 통한 인센티브 정상화를 통해 수익성 개선 지속할 계획 
 연간 가이던스 하향 조정: 글로벌 도매 판매 목표를 기존 416만대에서 
400만대로 하향 조정. 매출 성장률은 +1~2%, OPM 가이던스는 기존 
6.3~7.3%에서 5.5~6.5%로 하향 조정. 자동차 부문 투자 계획은 17.8조
원에서 16.0조원으로 축소. 핵심 R&D 투자는 유지하되 Capex 집행 시기 
및 우선 순위 조정할 계획 
 4분기 실적 반등 전망: 4분기 주요 신차(투싼, 아반떼, GV80 HEV, GV90) 
출시 본격화로 3분기까지의 부진을 만회할 수 있을 것으로 기대. 원가 부담 
은 비용 절감 활동을 통해 충분히 만회가 가능한 것으로 3분기 실적을 통
해 확인됨. 본격적인 주가 반등을 위해서는 판매 회복 이상의 주가 트리거 
필요할 것으로 보이나, 4분기 반전의 실마리가 나타나고 있다는 점에 주목. 
동사에 대한 투자의견 매수와 목표주가 60만원을 유지함

Compliance Notice 
당사는 자료 작성일 기준으로 지난 3 개월 간 해당종목에 대해서 유가증권 발행에 참여한 적이 없습니다 
당사는 본 자료 발간일을 기준으로 해당종목의 주식을 1% 이상 보유하고 있지 않습니다  
당사는 동 자료를 기관투자가 또는 제 3 자에게 사전 제공한 사실이 없습니다 
조사분석담당자는 자료작성일 현재 동 종목과 관련하여 재산적 이해관계가 없습니다 
동 자료에 게재된 내용들은 조사분석담당자 본인의 의견을 정확하게 반영하고 있으며, 외부의 부당한 압력이나 간섭 없이 작성되었음을 확인합니다 
동 자료는 당사의 제작물로서 모든 저작권은 당사에게 있습니다 
동 자료는 당사의 동의 없이 어떠한 경우에도 어떠한 형태로든 복제, 배포, 전송, 변형, 대여할 수 없습니다 
동 자료에 수록된 내용은 당사 리서치센터가 신뢰할 만한 자료 및 정보로부터 얻어진 것이나, 당사는 그 정확성이나 완전성을 보장할 수 없습니다. 따라서 어떠
한 경우에도 자료는 고객의 주식투자의 결과에 대한 법적 책임소재에 대한 증빙자료로 사용될 수 없습니다

기아 (000270/KS) 
라인업 확대의 성과와 주주환원에 주목 
2027 년 영업이익 7.96 조 원 전망 
2027 년 실적은 매출액 124.3 조 원(YoY +1.0%), 영업이익 7.96 조 원(YoY -
5.3%, OPM 6.4%), 지배주주순이익 6.97 조 원(YoY -0.2%)으로 전망한다. 우호적
이었던 환율 효과가 약해지는 가운데, 높아진 원재료 투입가격과 유럽을 중심으로 한 
판매 경쟁도 수익성에 부담으로 남는다. 판매를 지키기 위한 인센티브 확대까지 감안하
면 물량 증가가 그대로 이익 증가로 이어지기는 어렵다. 다만 이미 투입한 신차의 연간 
판매 기여와 미국, 유럽의 라인업 확대가 비용 부담을 일부 상쇄하면서, 하반기에는 전
년 동기 대비 증익으로 돌아설 것으로 본다.

지역별로 갖춘 제품, PBV 로 넓어지는 고객 
2027 년에는 이미 출시한 신차의 판매를 늘려 제품과 설비에 투자한 성과를 매출과 이
익으로 연결하는 일이 중요하다. 미국에서는 텔루라이드 등 주력 SUV 와 확대된 하이
브리드 차종으로 수요에 대응한다. 전기차 전환을 서두르지 않는 고객에게도 선택지를 
제공하며, 이 가운데 현지 생산 차종은 완성차 수입에 따른 관세 부담을 줄이는 데도 도
움이 된다. 유럽에서는 EV2·EV4 등 전기차와 신형 셀토스로 판매 기회를 넓힌다. 기
존 슬로바키아 공장을 활용해 전기차를 공급할 수 있지만, 중국 업체와의 경쟁 속에서 
판촉 부담을 관리하는 과제도 남아 있다.

PBV 는 PV5 에 이어 PV7 을 투입하며 물류 및 운송업체의 용도별 수요에 대응할 계
획이다. 차량 크기와 적재 수요에 맞춘 제품에 판매와 정비망을 더하면 고객과의 거래
를 넓힐 수 있다. 미국 하이브리드의 판매 확대, 유럽 전기차의 안착, PBV 의 고객 확보
는 각각 이익에 기여하는 속도가 다르다. 따라서 판매 증가와 함께 제품 구성의 변화, 
신규 차종의 초기 비용이 수익성에 미치는 영향을 살펴야 한다.

투자의견 매수, 목표주가 12 만 원으로 커버리지 개시 
투자의견 매수, 목표주가 12 만 원으로 커버리지를 개시한다. 당사 12 개월 선행 EPS 에 
최근 1 년 평균 P/E 6.5 배를 적용했다. 연간 감익 부담은 남아 있지만, 지역별 수요에 
대응할 제품과 생산기반은 확대돼 있다. 낮아진 밸류에이션 부담을 감안하면, 이미 갖
춘 라인업의 판매 성과와 총주주환원율 35% 이상의 정책에 주목해야 한다는 판단이
다. 
2026-11-25

모빌리티/피지컬AI. 권순우 / soonwoo@sks.co.kr / 02-3773-9592

Source PDF: all report pdf/[012450] 한화에어로스페이스(012450) 여전히 비중확대 의견, 그리고 Top-Pick한화에어로스페이스(012450) 여전히 비중확대 의견, 그리고 Top-Pick_cd366e43.pdf

### Page 1

(012450) 
한화에어로스페이스 
2026.10.30

AI 로보틱스/방산/조선/운송 양승윤 
6139 / syyang0901@eugenefn.com

여전히 비중확대 의견, 그리고 Top-Pick 
 투자의견 BUY(유지) 
 목표주가 1,660,000 원(유지) 
 현재주가 760,000 원(10/29)

 3Q26 연결 매출액은 6조 943억원(-6.0%yoy), 영업이익은 4,520억원(-
46.2%yoy, OPM 7.4%)을 기록. 영업이익 컨센 1조 1,614억원을 하회.  
 지상방산의 손익이 크게 악화. 영업이익은 217억원(OPM 1.7%) 기록. 주요 
납품이 4분기에 집중된 가운데 일회성 비용 약 500억원(대전사업장 복구·
정상화 약 300억원, 국내 사업 충당금 약 160억원)과 환율 영향이 겹침.   
 다만 내용을 살펴보면 수출 사업의 수익성 훼손은 크지 않았다는 판단임.  
 일회성 제외 영업이익은 약 717억원(OPM 5.8%)이며, 일회성 비용은 대전
사업장이 국내 물량을 전담한다는 점에서 전액 국내 귀속으로 추정. 내수 
OPM을 일회성 제외 -5% 내외로 가정하면, 내수 영업이익은 약 -826억원
(일회성 포함 OPM -12.7%), 수출은 1,043억원(OPM 17.6%)으로 추정됨. 
 1Q26 대비로는 국내 마진 하락이, 2Q26 대비로는 수출 물량 감소가 가장 
크게 작용(도표 참고). 국내는 저마진 사업 매출이 늘고 고마진 반복 양산 물
량이 줄며 일시적으로 믹스가 악화. 수출은 폴란드 등 주요 인도가 정체되어 
매출이 감소한 가운데 저수익 사업 중심으로 믹스가 악화된 영향. 
 4Q26부터 회복 구간 진입 전망. 폴란드 K9 2차 실행계약 물량의 현지 인
도가 본격화되며, 회사는 2026년 폴란드 K9 30문 이상, 천무 발사대 60대 
이상(*연초 40대에서 상향) 인도를 계획. 수출입 데이터상으로도 폴란드·루
마니아향 출하가 대규모로 진행 중이며 폴란드향 부속류 수출도 정상적으로 
이어지는 것으로 확인. 일회성 소멸과 고마진 수출 집중으로 수출 OPM은 
30% 내외로 회복할 것. 다만 국내는 정상 가동(2027년 상반기 목표) 전까
지 한 자릿수 초반 마진에 머물 것으로 전망. 
 중장기 수익성 우려도 크지 않다는 판단. 2027~2029년 매출 연 15% 성
장 가이던스는 유지됐고, 내년 폴란드 K9·천무 인도 물량은 모두 올해 대비 
증가할 계획. 폴란드 사업 이후 높아진 수출 마진 레벨은 후속 사업에서도 
유지될 전망. 수주는 중동 지정학 불안으로 지연되고 있으나 대공체계 등 문
의가 이어지고 있어, 2027년부터 중동이 수주의 중심이 될 것으로 기대. 
 동사에 대한 투자의견 BUY, 목표주가 166만원을 유지함. 3Q26 지상방산 
쇼크는 국내 적자와 수출 물량 공백이 겹친 일시적 결과로, 수출 경쟁력 훼
손으로 보기 어려움. 실적 충격에 따른 주가 조정은 분할 매수 기회로 판단.  
 한화에어로스페이스의 주가는 4월 7일 사상 최고가 153.7만원에서 10월 
29일 76.0만원으로 -51% 조정 받은 상황. 12MF P/E 기준 14.3x, 27E 
P/E는 13.9x로 밸류에이션 관점에서도 매력적인 구간 진입. 그동안 3분기 
실적 우려 등으로 부진했다면, 이제는 실적 바텀아웃과 대형 수주 랠리 속 
다시 한 번 반등을 기록할 차례. 동사를 방산업종 Top-pick으로 유지함.

Compliance Notice 
당사는 자료 작성일 기준으로 지난 3 개월 간 해당종목에 대해서 유가증권 발행에 참여한 적이 없습니다 
당사는 본 자료 발간일을 기준으로 해당종목의 주식을 1% 이상 보유하고 있지 않습니다  
당사는 동 자료를 기관투자가 또는 제 3 자에게 사전 제공한 사실이 없습니다 
조사분석담당자는 자료작성일 현재 동 종목과 관련하여 재산적 이해관계가 없습니다 
동 자료에 게재된 내용들은 조사분석담당자 본인의 의견을 정확하게 반영하고 있으며, 외부의 부당한 압력이나 간섭 없이 작성되었음을 확인합니다 
동 자료는 당사의 제작물로서 모든 저작권은 당사에게 있습니다 
동 자료는 당사의 동의 없이 어떠한 경우에도 어떠한 형태로든 복제, 배포, 전송, 변형, 대여할 수 없습니다 
동 자료에 수록된 내용은 당사 리서치센터가 신뢰할 만한 자료 및 정보로부터 얻어진 것이나, 당사는 그 정확성이나 완전성을 보장할 수 없습니다. 따라서 어떠
한 경우에도 자료는 고객의 주식투자의 결과에 대한 법적 책임소재에 대한 증빙자료로 사용될 수 없습니다

투자기간 및 투자등급/투자의견 비율 
종목추천 및 업종추천 투자기간: 12 개월  (추천기준일 종가대비 추천종목의 예상 목표수익률을 의미함) 당사 투자의견 비율(%)

ㆍHOLD(중립) 추천기준일 종가대비 -10%이상 ∼ +15%미만 3%

### Page 1

매수 (유지)  
악조건 속에서도 이 정도 
HD현대중공업의 2026년 3분기 영업실적은 매출액 5조 7,536억원(yoy +30.2%, qoq 
`-9.1%), 영업이익 8,992억원(yoy +61.3%, qoq -13.5%)으로 당사 추정치에 대체로 
부합하였다. 조업일수 감소와 원화 강세로 매출액이 감소했으며, 임단협 타결로 
인한 성과금 및 격려금이 당분기에 비용으로 반영되었다. 해양플랜트 부문에서는 
RUYA 프로젝트의 공사 진행률 확대와 TRION 프로젝트의 C/O(계약 변경) 
180억원이 추가로 반영되며 매출액이 전분기대비 0.7% 증가하였다. 엔진기계 
부문은 환율 하락을 판가 상승과 D/F(이중연료) 비중 증가로 상쇄하며 전분기와 
유사한 영업실적을 달성했다. 
2027년에도 지속될 수주 시점 효과 
3분기 대형선 야드의 수주 시점별 매출 비중은 2022년 4%, 2023년 45%, 2024년 
31%, 2025년 20% 수준으로 파악된다. 상대적으로 선가가 낮았던 2022~2023년 
물량이 여전히 매출의 절반 가량을 차지하고 있는 셈이다. 따라서 2027년 말까지도 
고선가 물량의 매출 비중 확대가 이어질 것으로 예상된다. 이는 LNG운반선 매출 
비중 감소에도 불구하고 수익성을 방어할 수 있는 핵심 요인으로 작용할 전망이다.   
투자의견 매수 유지, 목표주가 550,000원으로 하향 
투자의견은 매수로 유지하나, 목표주가는 550,000원으로 34.5% 하향한다. 환율 
가정을 기존 1,450원 수준에서 1,400원으로 조정함에 따라 목표주가 산정의 기준이 
되는 2027~2028년 실적추정치를 하향조정하였다. 또한 미국 네오 클라우드 
업체들의 자금 조달 여건 악화로 데이터센터 증설이 지연되고 있어, 발전용 엔진 
증설을 근거로 동사에 부여하였던 프리미엄을 제거하여 P/E 배수를 19.8배로 
하향조정했다. 그러나 본업인 상선 부문은 향후 고부가가치 가스선의 발주 강세를 
전망하기 때문에 투자의견은 매수로 유지한다.

Document 303: Cliff Notes_ in pursuit of future gain (2026-11-27)

Source PDF: all report pdf/Cliff Notes_ in pursuit of future gain (2026-11-27).pdf
Report source: Westpac IQ
Date: 2026-11-27
Pages: 10
Status: ok

### Page 1

#cliffnotes #rba #australia #inflation #us #fomc #ecb #aud #usd
Cliff Notes: in pursuit of future gain
By Ryan Wells, Elliot Clarke & Illiana Jain
10:02 November 27 2026 Share
Key insights from the week that was.
The main event in Australia this week was the October CPI report which surprised modestly to the
upside, headline inflation rising 0.5% (4.9%yr). The main drivers were auto fuel (+6.2%), domestic
holiday travel (+4.5%) and new dwelling costs (+0.8%). Fuelled by some project home builders

reducing discounts, the increase in new dwelling costs was noteworthy; however, subdued demand
should see discounting return. More broadly, underlying inflation continued to accelerate. Trimmed

### Page 2

mean inflation gained 0.5% in the month and 3.8%yr, a cycle high that breaks the 3.5–3.6%yr range
held since May. The six-month annualised pulse was stronger still at 4.3%yr.
As noted by Westpac Chief Economist Luci Ellis following the release, October likely marks the peak
for inflation, but the upside surprise in dwellings and other early-quarter components skews the risks to
our Q4 trimmed mean estimate of 0.9%qtr up. The RBA is unlikely to relax its focus on price risks until

well into the new year.
This week, Australia also received two partial indicators for investment ahead of next Wednesday’s Q3
GDP report.
Construction activity was in line with Westpac’s expectation, up 0.9% in Q3 and 5.8% over the year.
The detail shows a clear rotation in activity: growth in infrastructure work moderating (1.3%yr) as major
public projects roll off; while momentum in residential (9.3%yr) and non-residential construction
(10.2%yr) pick up. Further, a strong pipeline of projects remains in place, ready to soak up any

additional capacity released by public infrastructure completions.
Private CAPEX subsequently surprised to the upside, up 12.2% in Q3, surpassing Westpac’s top-of-
the-market forecast of 10%. The headline result was driven by another very strong showing for data
centre-related investment, represented by a 184% surge in machinery and equipment spending in
information media and telecommunications. There are also signs of a nascent uptrend in investment

forming across other sectors, including utilities, financial and insurance services, and retail trade. At
16%yr, real (inflation-adjusted) capex plans for 2026-27 are the strongest since the mining investment
boom.
Ahead of next Wednesday’s release, our Q3 GDP preview will be published later today on Westpac IQ.
Offshore this week, the data released corroborated known themes. Ahead of Thanksgiving, US Q3
GDP growth was revised up from 3.4% annualised to 4.3% in the second estimate. Underlying the

result was stronger growth in personal consumption, revised up from 3.8% annualised to 4.2%, and a
broad-based upgrade for business investment. That said, monthly PCE data suggests consumer
momentum waned at quarter end, with real spending up just 0.1% in September and unchanged in
October despite an acceleration in nominal personal income growth (from 0.3% to 0.7%).
The Federal Reserve's Beige Book remains more downbeat than the hard data. "Eight of the twelve

Federal Reserve Districts reported growth in the slight to moderate range, while four Districts reported
no change. Consumer spending was little changed, as households remained price sensitive and were
increasingly selective in discretionary purchases." Given weak population growth and uncertainty over
momentum outside the tech sector, employers are " focused on utilizing existing staff more efficiently”,
“with many firms reporting stable head counts and hiring primarily to replace for attrition”. These

findings suggest that, should the pace of expansion in AI infrastructure plateau or turn, US GDP could
quickly return near trend.
The market’s mood continues to depend not only on the data to hand but also ongoing threats to
Middle East oil supply, Houthi rebels struck Saudi assets again this week with limited effect, and

### Page 3

Related articles
expectations for the AI buildout, which this week was dominated by news around NVIDIA. US trade
policy also remains a threat, the Trump administration this week announcing plans for a fresh round of
tariffs on China, the European Union and several other major trading partners to replace previous
levies struck down by the Supreme Court. China’s 20% tariff could double to 40% should trade
negotiations break down. India may also face a 25% tariff if it fails to reach a trade agreement with the

US. Uncertainty is likely to persist on each front well into 2027.

Document 309: Construction rotates from infrastructure to housing and data centres (2026-11-27)

Source PDF: all report pdf/Construction rotates from infrastructure to housing and data centres (2026-11-27).pdf
Report source: Westpac IQ
Date: 2026-11-27
Pages: 18
Status: ok

### Page 1

#australia #construction
Construction rotates from infrastructure to housing and data
centres
By Pat Bustamante
Senior Economist, Westpac
14:36 November 25 2026 Share
Construction activity rose 0.9%qtr in Q3 and 5.8%yr. Construction activity remained solid,
driven by residential building (+3.1%qtr), non-residential building (+1.5%qtr, includes data
centres), partly offset by engineering construction (-0.9%qtr).

### Page 2

Construction activity rose 0.9%qtr in Q3 2026, taking annual growth to a strong 5.8%. The quarterly
increase was close to Westpac Economics’ 0.8%qtr forecast, but well above the 0.4%qtr market
consensus. Importantly, sizeable upward revisions to private construction lifted annual growth by
almost 2ppts. These revisions suggest the economy was somewhat stronger in 2025–26 than
previously thought, despite the same observed inflation outcomes.

What does this mean for the near-term outlook?
Overall, today’s data show construction activity continuing to expand at a solid pace, although there are
important offsetting forces beneath the headline result. Public infrastructure work remains soft and is
expected to ease further as major east-coast projects reach completion. This is being partly offset by
public non-residential construction, particularly health, schools and other social infrastructure.
At the same time, private construction remains strong. Residential construction is up 9.3% over the
year, the commercial building pipeline, which includes data centres, is around record highs, and the
renewable-energy investment pipeline remains substantial as we work towards the 82% renewable
electricity target by 2030.
Taken together, demand in the construction sector is likely to remain elevated in the near term, but its

### Page 3

composition is shifting. The pullback in public infrastructure is freeing up labour and other construction
capacity for housing, data centres, renewables and commercial projects. This rebalancing has
already been substantial, with our estimates suggesting the government footprint in the
construction industry declined by around 1.5ppts in 2025–26 (Government’s footprint plateaus as
services rise and construction retreats).
What does this mean for the National Accounts?

Today’s release is the first of a set of partials which will feed into next week’s September quarter 2026
National Accounts. Today’s numbers alone point to stronger growth in residential and new buildings
activity over the quarter and some upside for our Q3 estimate. However, with several important partial
indicators still to come, we will provide our full assessment in Friday’s GDP preview.
What about inflationary pressures and the impact of the data centre boom?

The spike in construction costs during the pandemic was largely synchronised across dwellings, non-
residential buildings and engineering construction. Today, however, cost pressures differ as demand
profiles across these segments diverge.
While rebounding in the quarter, engineering construction costs have been softer as major public
infrastructure programs wind down. In contrast, dwelling construction costs are accelerating as activity
in this segment picks up. Non-residential building costs appear to be going sideways, rather than

significantly reaccelerating, despite the strength in private investment, including data centres.

### Page 4

At 4.5%yr, aggregate construction cost growth is above what was recorded over 2018-19. This raises
questions about whether the strong demand in pockets like data centre investment are contributing to
broader inflationary pressures. For now, there is limited evidence of this happening. However,
construction cost deflators continue to firm in year-ended terms, suggesting that the disinflation
anticipated by the RBA may take longer to materialise.
How this plays out going forward depends on how much the substantial roll-off in public construction

activity makes room for the pickup in private residential and non-residential building work, particularly
data centres, and in private engineering construction associated with the renewable energy transition.
Total construction work yet to be done has risen to around 9.2% of nominal GDP in Q2 2026, compared
with an average of around 8.8% since 2022.
If that pipeline continues to build faster than public infrastructure releases capacity,
construction-sector cost pressures could prove more persistent than currently anticipated.

However, this is not our central case, as higher interest rates and ongoing cost increases are
likely to dampen private demand, while the public infrastructure pipeline continues to wind
down.

### Page 5

The detail
Residential construction outperformed, rising 3.1%qtr in Q3, following an upwardly revised 2.8%qtr
increase in Q2 2026 (previously estimated at 1.7%qtr). Growth was driven by a 3.9%qtr increase in
private residential construction, its strongest quarterly gain since June 2005, outside the COVID period.
In year-ended terms, residential construction is up 9.3%yr, the strongest growth rate since the
September quarter of 2016. This suggests the sector is responding to growing housing demand by

expanding output despite ongoing cost pressures. Some of this strength may also reflect the pullback
in public construction activity, which is freeing up labour and other resources for residential and non-
residential projects.
The strength in residential construction suggests the sector is responding more meaningfully to
Australia's housing shortage. However, with approvals likely to soften as higher interest rates bite and
demand moderates, the current pace of expansion may prove difficult to sustain.

### Page 6

Engineering construction (EC) declined 0.9%qtr in Q3 2026 but was 1.3%higher in year-ended
terms.
As foreshadowed in this month’s Market Outlook, the public infrastructure pipeline appears to have
passed its peak, as major transport projects across Australia’s east coast, particularly in NSW and
Victoria, move towards completion. Consistent with this, public engineering construction declined
3.0%year in Q3 but stabilised over the quarter to post a gain of 2.9%qtr. More broadly, the trend

remains weak, with activity falling in five of the past seven quarters.

### Page 7

In contrast, private engineering construction remains robust, up almost 5% over the year. However,
activity fell 3.7%qtr in Q3, suggesting some moderation may also be emerging in this segment. One
area of continued strength is electricity infrastructure, with the value of private work on electricity
generation and distribution rising by an estimated 10%qtr in seasonally adjusted and nominal terms,
largely reflecting renewable energy projects such as wind and solar developments.

Looking ahead, the private engineering pipeline remains supported by structural investment themes,
particularly the energy transition and nationwide rollout of data centres. However, constraints around
utility provision, grid connections and supporting infrastructure remain a key risk to the timely delivery
of these projects.
Non-residential building construction increased 1.5%qtr in Q3 2026, leaving activity 10.2% higher
over the year. Private-sector activity was mixed, declining 0.4%qtr, but remained 11% higher in year-

ended terms. Public activity also picked up sharply, rising 6.2%qtr to be 8.3% higher over the year,
partly reflecting investment in health and other facilities required to service a growing population.
Overall, non-residential construction remains strong, supported by two key investment themes: the
rollout of data centres in the private sector and increased public investment in health and other social
infrastructure, including in Queensland.

### Page 8

Although the contribution from individual projects is difficult to quantify, the surge in data centre
commencements during the second half of 2025 is likely supporting activity and should remain an
important source of construction demand over the second half of this year and beyond.

### Page 9

State detail
Construction activity increased across four of the six states in Q3, although outcomes varied
considerably.
South Australia recorded the strongest quarterly increase, rising 3.9%qtr, followed by NSW (+2.2%qtr)
and Victoria (+0.8%qtr). In contrast, activity declined in Western Australia (–1.9%qtr) and Queensland
(–1.2%qtr).
Despite the quarterly volatility, construction activity remained higher than a year ago across all states.
Tasmania recorded the strongest annual growth (+17.3%yr), followed by South Australia (+11.3%yr),

NSW (+8.1%yr), Western Australia (+6.4%yr), Victoria (+4.4%yr) and Queensland (+2.6%yr).

### Page 1

It is hard to know what to make of Monday's surprise
dollar strength. Blaming fresh unhedged inﬂows into the US
tech sector following White House intervention on Nvidia is not
that appealing as a rationale. But it may be the case. We think
the dollar is starting to head into overshoot territory and would
be careful about expecting more strength just yet

Chris Turner, Frantisek Taborsky and Francesco Pesole
USD: Strange price action on Monday
It was hard to keep up with FX market price action on Monday. The
dollar seemed bid most of the day, but saw a strong leg higher
when it was announced that Jensen Huang was rejoining Nvidia
and the Nasdaq surged. One could ask: if the dollar did not fall
during last week's tech sell-oﬀ, why should it rally now? It is hard to
know the driving forces behind short-term dollar moves, but the
dollar has certainly overshot short-term rate diﬀerentials.
In recent weeks, we ﬂoated a DXY target of 104.60, which has just
about been hit. Given the prospect of a hot October core PCE print

Document 352: India_ A middle class too small to supplant China’s economic role (2026-10-26)

Source PDF: all report pdf/India_ A middle class too small to supplant China’s economic role (2026-10-26).pdf
Report source: BNP Paribas
Date: 2026-10-26
Pages: 8
Status: ok

### Page 1

The bank
for a changing
world
At a time when Chinese consumption is slowing significantly, could India become a driver of global consumption? For now, this
prospect presents considerable difficulties, with many obstacles to overcome. Despite dynamic growth and the Indian economy’s
expanding share of global output (8.2% of worldwide GDP in 2025), the middle-class consumer population remains modest and is nowhere
near the size of China’s.
This is due to the concentration of employment in low-value-added and largely informal sectors. The few manufacturing sectors with

high value-added and strong job potential — electrical equipment, electronics, automotive, chemicals and pharmaceuticals — continue to
face constraints from an unfavourable business environment and a skills shortage that is holding back their move into premium market
segments.
Furthermore, the highest-paying service sector jobs, particularly in information technology, are now threatened by the rise of
artificial intelligence. Without concrete measures, the “demographic window” — which is expected to remain favourable for another 15 to

25 years — could close before India establishes a solid industrial base and a sufficiently large middle class.
INDIA: A MIDDLE CLASS TOO SMALL TO SUPPLANT CHINA’S ECONOMIC ROLE
Johanna Melka
issue 20
26 October 2026

### Page 2

2
economic-research.bnpparibas.comEcoInsight 20 / 26 October 2026
The bank
for a changing
worldINDIA: A MIDDLE CLASS TOO SMALL TO SUPPLANT CHINA’S ECONOMIC ROLE
INDIA IS STRUGGLING TO ESTABLISH A ROBUST MIDDLE
CLASS
With an annual growth rate of 7.3% over the past decade (excluding the
pandemic), India has one of the highest growth rates among the world’s
major economies. Yet, its middle-class consumer base remains surpri-
singly small. When measured in current U.S. dollars, total consumption

is very high in India (USD 2,241 billion in 2025), but this figure prima-
rily reflects the country’s overall population size. By comparison, this
consumption level falls 3.5 times short of China’s corresponding figure.
Furthermore, per capita consumption adjusted for purchasing power
parity (PPP) is 1.3 times lower than that of Indonesia, the Philippines
and Vietnam. When evaluated at constant prices, consumption per ca-
pita has increased only slightly over the past thirty years compared to

China and even Vietnam (Chart 1).
According to our estimates, based on the research conducted by Ló-
pez-Calva and Ortiz-Juarez (2014)1, India’s middle-class consumer
segment accounts for just 1.6% of the total population (see Table 1),
or fewer than 24 million people. This is less than in Thailand and Viet-
nam (more than 30 million each) and far below China (approximately
490 million, according to the same definition).
The majority of households identified as middle class by standard In-

dian research institutions (the Centre for Monitoring Indian Economy
[CMIE] and the National Council of Applied Economic Research [NCAER])
would, in fact, be categorised as “vulnerable” according to World Bank
criteria: households that have officially emerged from poverty yet lack
the financial stability necessary for sustained economic security.
Highly concentrated consumption and limited
upward mobility
The structure of Indian household consumption reveals significant

disparities between urban and rural areas. In 2024, average month-
ly consumption per capita amounted to 6,996 rupees in urban areas
1 These authors define the middle class as individuals for whom the risk of falling back into poverty is limited (less than 4% over four years), corresponding to daily per capita income
ranging from 17 to 98 2021 PPP dollars (with vulnerability thresholds at 6.85 and 14 2017 PPP dollars, according to the World Bank). In the absence of reliable national income

data collection mechanisms in India, our study applies these thresholds to the World Bank’s consumer expenditure data, an approach aligned with the objective of assessing actual
purchasing capacity deployed in the market (although this tends to slightly underestimate the middle class in terms of income, as household savings remain unaccounted for in
expenditure measurements).
(approximately USD 84.5), compared with 4,122 rupees in rural areas
(less than USD 50). The wealthiest 5% of urban consumers spent an

average equivalent of USD 245 per month per person. Consequently,
discretionary spending power remained concentrated among the hi-
ghest income brackets.
The structure of consumption has changed over the past decade. The
proportion allocated to food purchases (34.8% of total spending on ave-
rage) declined by 4.3 percentage points between 2011–12 and 2023–
24, while allocations to transportation and communications increased
to 12.4% (+3.8 pp). Although the allocation to durable goods has in-

creased, it remains below 7% of total household spending. This increase
was driven more by a rise in the use of credit than by income growth.
Over the past ten years, consumer loans have been the main source of
household debt. Although still modest relative to GDP, household debt
rose by 12.2 pp to reach 47.8% of GDP by the end of 2025, according to
the Bank for International Settlements (BIS).

THE LABOUR MARKET SUFFERS FROM STRUCTURAL
WEAKNESSES
The limited size of the middle class and the low level of household
consumption in India can be attributed, in particular, to the enduring
structural weaknesses of the labour market. The labour market faces
two major problems: insufficient job creation and the fact that most of
the jobs created are low-skilled and low-paid.

Insufficient job creation
Despite the Indian economy’s robust growth, its employment rate re-
mains substantially lower than in comparable Asian economies: 52.7%
in 2025 according to the International Labour Organization (ILO), com-
pared with 65.6% in Indonesia and 71.3% in Vietnam. This disparity
is mainly due to low labour force participation among women (34%
compared with 53.4% and 68%) and young people (40.8% of those aged
20-24 compared with 66.7% and 69.4%).

Over the past ten years, employment growth has averaged just 2.4%
annually, whereas GDP growth was close to 6% per year.
The services sector (representing 33.2% of employment in 2025) ac-
counted for the bulk of job creation during this period (+3.2% per year),
far ahead of the manufacturing sector (+1.4%) and, above all, agricultu-
re (+0.1%), which nevertheless remains the country’s largest employer
(41.5% of the workforce). This is not enough to absorb the 10 to 12 mil-

lion new entrants to the labour market each year.
2 Employment elasticity remained relatively stable between 1990 and 2012.
A limited number of sectors have significant po-
tential for creating high-quality, well-paid jobs
To identify sectors with high potential for job creation, we calculate
employment elasticity both for aggregate economy (KLEMS data) and
for the formal manufacturing sector alone (Annual Survey of Industries,
2025).
Over the period 1981–2023, aggregate employment elasticity remained

low at a virtually unchanged level, close to 0.3. This stability, however,
masks significant sectoral divergences: elasticity remained stable in
the services sector throughout the period (0.5), but it declined shar-
ply in the manufacturing sector (from 0.4 during 1981–1990 to just
0.1 during 2012–20232): at the same growth rate, India now creates
far fewer industrial jobs per unit of growth than it did forty years ago
(Table 2). In the formal manufacturing sector alone, employment elas-

ticity reached 0.7 over the past ten years, driven by very robust job
creation. However, this positive trend has not been sufficient to offset
the decline in informal sector employment, which accounts for 73.4%
of total manufacturing employment but only 38% of the sector’s value
added. Labour productivity in the formal manufacturing sector is thus
estimated to be 5.4 times higher than in the informal sector.
In capital-intensive sectors, employment elasticity ranged from 0.5 to

0.6 (including electrical equipment, transportation, chemicals and me-
tals), whereas in labour-intensive sectors — specifically wood, textiles
and apparel, and manufacturing food — employment elasticities re-
mained particularly low, or in certain cases, negative.

world
In other words, growth in value added in these sectors with high labour 
absorption potential is primarily driven by productivity gains rather 
than job creation. Furthermore, even among companies operating in 
the formal sector, employment elasticity remains low at an average of 
0.4. Consequently, the traditional mechanism facilitating labour mobi-
lity from the agricultural sector to the labour-intensive manufacturing 
sector has been very limited over the past forty years.
The only manufacturing subsectors that combine strong growth, high 
employment elasticity, and high productivity — thereby offering real 
potential for creating high-quality jobs — are electronics and infor-
mation technology, electrical equipment, the automotive industry, the 
chemical industry, and, to a lesser extent, the pharmaceutical industry. 
In these subsectors, the formal employment elasticity ranges from 0.8 
to 1.2 (Chart 2). However, in 2025, these jobs accounted for just 1.4% 
of total employment and 5.9% of GDP. The potential therefore remains 
negligible at the macroeconomic level.
Several structural constraints limit job creation in 
India
- The high rigidity of the labour market: it consists mainly of small, in-
formal businesses — those with fewer than 50 employees account for 
65% of the productive sector — largely due to the stringency of labour 
legislation (for example, companies with more than 300 employees 
cannot carry out mass layoffs without authorisation).
- Numerous obstacles impede the growth of industrial and job-crea-
ting businesses: an unfavourable business environment (particularly 
difficulties in accessing land acquisition to build factories); high costs 
of accessing capital (particularly for small and medium-sized enter-
prises); insufficient export competitiveness in labour-intensive sectors 
(textiles, clothing), which encounter disadvantages, including less ad-
vantageous tariff conditions3 than competing Asian economies.
- The mismatch between the skills of the available workforce and the 
needs of businesses. This mismatch constrains wage growth and the 
development of household purchasing power, obstructs industrial ad-
vancement, and, consequently, the creation of high-quality jobs.
INADEQUATE JOB QUALITY DUE TO A LACK OF  
APPROPRIATE SKILLS
A predominantly informal and low-paid labour  
market
According to the ILO, 87.2% of total employment in India is informal, 
with 73.4% concentrated in the manufacturing sector alone, predomi-
nantly engaged in low-value-added activities that require few skills. 
Furthermore, the Periodic Labour Force Survey (PLFS) 2023/24 indi-
cates that 58.4% of Indian workers are self-employed, 19.8% are day 
labourers, and only 21.7% are regular employees — the highest-paying 
status, with a wage gap of more than 60% compared to the other two 
categories. According to the ILO, 20% of the population captures 79% of 
aggregate labour income. The agricultural sector, which still employs 
41.5% of the labour force while contributing less than 15% to GDP, is

3 Historically, tariff rates imposed on Indian products exported to Europe have been higher than those applied to certain other Asian countries (Bangladesh, Vietnam, Pakistan).
4 According to the World Bank and the NCAER, educational and training programmes are too theoretical and fail to develop cognitive and interpersonal skills. Vocational education 
and training are not designed to meet the needs of high-demand sectors. Collaborative relationships between educational institutions and employers — particularly regarding 
internships and on-the-job training — remain inadequately developed.
3.7 times less productive than the services sector, with a median hourly 
wage that is 40 to 50% lower.
A widespread skills shortage
This concentration of earnings reflects the low skill levels of the jobs 
held: according to the PLFS, 88.2% of workers hold jobs requiring little 
or no skill. The proportion of skilled jobs is just 11.2% and has declined 
by 4.6 pp since 2018. The underlying challenge is not a shortage of 
graduates but rather a misalignment between what the labour market 
needs and the educational preparation provided to graduates4, which 
results in their gradual downward mobility into positions far below 
their formal qualifications.
Unemployment, the downward mobility of graduates, and a shortage 
of technical skills
This disparity between skills and business needs directly contributes to 
unemployment among graduates, which reached 13.5% in 2025 accor-
ding to the ILO, compared with 1.3% for individuals without a degree. 
Within the 25- to 34-year-old demographic, the unemployment rate 
stands at 15.2% for graduates (19.2% for those with a master’s de-
gree), compared with an average of 6.1% across this age group. Only 
8.3% of graduates secure positions that genuinely correspond to their 
educational attainment; 53% of bachelor’s degree holders and 36% of 
master’s degree holders are overqualified for their positions.
In low-skilled jobs, the lack of technical training represents a signifi-
cant barrier to youth employment: according to the World Bank’s En-
terprise Survey (2022), 17.8% of traditional service companies and 14% 
of “intermediate” manufacturing companies cite this as a major obsta-
cle. In India’s manufacturing sector, 32.9% of positions are reportedly 
held by underqualified workers, according to the ILO, compared with 
28.8% in Indonesia and just 16.5% in Vietnam.
A direct impediment to industrial advancement
A survey conducted by the Ministry of Skill Development & Entrepre-
neurship across seven key sectors — automotive, textiles, agriculture, 
livestock, IT, commerce and green energy — reveals that the pace of

The bank
for a changing
world
workforce training development significantly lags behind industry re-
quirements. This forces companies to train a considerable number of
young graduates themselves before they are ready for the workforce.
According to the Telecom Talent in the 5G Era report by the Telecom
Sector Skill Council, only 40% of IT and telecommunications graduates
are actually immediately employable.

This disparity in skills particularly constrains the transition towards
higher-value-added manufacturing activities within the sectors iden-
tified above (automotive, electronics and chemicals). Their growth
trajectory depends on technological advancement — electric vehicles,
semiconductors, specialty chemicals — and therefore on intermediate
to advanced technical skills that are currently lacking. Should trai-
ning provision fail to align with industry demand, employment elasti-

city and growth in these sectors will stagnate or decline, undermining
the only source of high-quality job creation currently available — and
consequently, the Indian economy’s ability to expand its middle class.
These structural limitations affecting national industrial development
diminish its appeal to foreign investors, despite its size. The stock of
foreign investment in India stood at USD 560 billion in Q2 2026, repre-
senting just 15% of GDP (compared with 20% in China, more than 52%

in Vietnam, and around 67% of GDP in Thailand and Malaysia).
ARTIFICIAL INTELLIGENCE: A NEW RISK TO JOB CREATION
Artificial intelligence (AI) may also limit the expansion of high-paid
employment opportunities in the service sector. Its deployment poses
a significant threat to employment in India, particularly in the hi-
ghest-paying service sectors — the same sectors that possessed si-
gnificant potential for expanding India’s middle-class consumer base.

Agriculture (41.5% of the labour force), construction (12%), and trade/
transportation (18%) remain largely immune to the direct replacement
of workers by AI, which reduces the overall macroeconomic scale of
the coming shock. The service sectors offering the most lucrative em-
ployment — IT, finance and insurance — account for just 1.3% and 1.2%
of total employment, yet these remain the primary targets of automa-
tion. In IT services companies, the adoption of AI is expected to reduce

the need for entry-level roles (quality assurance, software testing and
support roles) but increase demand for positions centred on platform
development and management oversight. Current sector-specific evi-
dence indicates a notable slowdown in job creation within IT services
but no widespread contraction. A study by the Indian Council for Re-
search on International Economic Relations (ICRIER) on the IT sector
confirms a decline in job creation for routine and entry-level roles.

While new professions are emerging such as data centre operators, AI
DevOps engineers who serve as intermediaries between software deve-
lopment and systems operations teams, and ethical AI specialists, the
volume of employment opportunities generated remains substantially
lower than the number of jobs at risk. Furthermore, the skill require-
ments for these newly created positions exceed those possessed by the
existing cohort of graduates.
REFORMS ARE A STEP IN THE RIGHT DIRECTION BUT DO

NOT GO FAR ENOUGH
Since 2019, the Modi government has implemented a series of mea-
sures to develop industry, attract foreign direct investment, and gain
greater integration in global trade (after decades of protectionist po-
licies).
Corporate subsidy programmes (Production-Linked Incentives) have
helped support the development of those sectors with the greatest
potential for job creation (electrical, electronics, chemicals, and auto-
motive). Similarly, labour market reform (which came into force in late

2025) may alleviate impediments to business expansion and the crea-
tion of formal jobs. However, its actual impact will depend on its adop-
tion by the states, which retain considerable discretionary autonomy
over labour law, and on its effective implementation on the ground.
The free trade agreement signed between India and the European
Union in late January 2026 (which is expected to take effect in 2027 at
the earliest) should mitigate the tariff disadvantage experienced by la-

bour-intensive industries: the EU has pledged to remove tariffs, which
currently range from 4% to 12% on textiles and clothing.
However, these measures alone will be inadequate for creating
high-quality jobs if the education system fails to meet the needs of
Indian businesses. It is therefore imperative that India continues to
improve its business environment and, even more importantly, reform
its education system to promote job creation in high-value-added and

well-paying sectors. Without concrete measures, the “demographic
window” — which is expected to remain favourable for another 15 to
25 years — could close before India establishes a robust industrial
foundation and a substantial middle class.

*****
While India’s economic growth has demonstrated considerable dy-
namism, this growth has not translated into the development of the
middle class. The real medium-term concern involves the emergence
of a “neo-precarious” middle class — substantial in terms of num-
bers but lacking the foundations necessary for advancing towards a
high-income economy (households with stable employment, the ability

to save, and social protection). The creation of formal, high-quality jobs
is currently inadequate to sustain industrial expansion and its progres-
sion towards higher-value segments. The cultivation of certain niche
activities, while promising, remains very limited and is far from benefi-
ting the entire population. Finally, the development of AI poses more of
a risk than a genuine opportunity for the country’s development. India
lacks both the infrastructure and the educational system necessary to

deploy AI in a way that would yield tangible benefits for its economy
and labour market.
Article completed on 19 October 2026

WESTPAC ECONOMICS
TODAY’S INSIGHTS
Financial Markets
•  Share markets remained under pressure even as official 
activity data surprised to upside, with traders rotating out 
of tech stocks as the new round of tariffs were formally 
announced. The rotation saw the NASDAQ (–0.8%) and S&P 
500 (–0.3%) fall while the Dow Jones gained 0.4%. Stocks 
were in the red across the pond, with the Euro Stoxx (–1.8%) 
and FTSE 100 (–0.4%) both down. Asia was weaker too, 
with declines in Tokyo (–2.8%), Hong Kong (–1.1%), Shanghai 
(–1.7%) and Seoul (–6.2%). The ASX 200 also slipped –0.4%, 
but futures are pointing to a more positive open this morning.
•  Treasuries continued to receive a safe-haven bid, driving a 
bull steepening as the 2Y fell 5bps to 4.64% while the 10Y 
fell 8bps to 4.85%. Yields were also lower across much of the 
globe, including the 10Y Bund (–4bps), 10Y JGB (–10bps) and 
10Y Gilt (–5bps). ACGB yields also fell following hotter-than-
expected inflation data, with 3Y and 10Y futures yields down 
3bps each to 4.43% and 4.80% respectively. Swaps markets 
are still pricing in the first RBA rate cut by August, though are 
less certain about the full quantum of moves into year-end.
•  The USD gave up early gains and weakened overnight, the 
DXY down 0.2% to around 103.7. The Euro (+0.1%) and 
Sterling (+0.1%) benefitted most from the move, while the 
Yen held broadly steady. The Aussie dollar bucked the wider 
trend, instead slipping 0.2% despite hotter-than-expected 
inflation data.
•  Oil prices rose slightly overnight as tensions in the Middle 
East persist, even with evidence of stronger global supply. 
Brent rose 3.3% to US$94.4/bbl. LME copper slipped another 
1.5% amid fragile risk sentiment, pulling prices to a new cycle 
low. Gold prices were broadly steady at around US$3,550/oz. 
International Data
In the US, personal income growth bounced back in October, 
accelerating from 0.3% in September to 0.7% (vs. expectations 
of 0.4%). Growth in personal spending was a touch weaker 
than expected in October at 0.3%, but the disappointment 
was offset by a small upward revision to September to 0.6%. 
Consumption growth over September and October was the 
result of price growth, however, with real spending up just 0.1% 
in September and unchanged in October. Headline and core 
PCE inflation met expectations at 0.3% in the month, edging 
annual inflation up to 3.8%yr and 3.3%yr as expected. 
US Q3 GDP growth (all figures annualised) was revised up from 
3.4% to 4.3% in the second estimate. Underlying the result was 
stronger growth in personal consumption, revised up from 3.8% 
to 4.2%, and a broad-based lift across business investment 
-- particularly non-residential construction (data centres and 
related infrastructure) and intellectual property. Into 2027, the 
pace at which the existing pipeline of data centre construction 
is worked through and global adoption of AI takes place will be 
critical for sustaining GDP above trend. 
Growth in durable goods orders disappointed in October, with 
activity up just 0.2% following a 2.0% increase in September. 
Core orders (ex air and defence) continued to show greater 
stability, rising 1.2% in October after a 0.8% gain in September. 
Equipment investment growth continues to be buoyed by data 
centre fit out. Meanwhile, new home sales continued to exhibit 
heightened volatility, rising 14.2% in October after a 9.7% 
decline in September. The current level is marginally above 
the average of the past 5 years; but also that of the past few 
decades, when the population was much smaller. And initial 
jobless claims edged back down last week from 202k to 189k.

The Federal Reserve’s Beige Book remained more downbeat 
than recent hard data. “Eight of the twelve Federal Reserve 
Districts reported growth in the slight to moderate range, while 
four Districts reported no change. Consumer spending was 
little changed, as households remained price sensitive and were 
increasingly selective in discretionary purchases.” “Residential 
real estate activity weakened as affordability challenges 
suppressed demand, while commercial real estate activity 
was mixed. Construction activity [also] declined amid higher 
borrowing costs and elevated construction costs.”

On the labour market, “Employment levels rose slightly on 
balance, with many firms reporting stable head counts and 
hiring primarily to replace for attrition, while only a minority 
noted meaningful increases”. “Employers focused on utilizing 
existing staff more efficiently, even as some added workers to 
support expansion.” Considering inflation, “Prices increased 
moderately overall, as in the prior reporting period”. “Input 
cost growth generally outpaced increases in selling prices, as 
competitive pressures and customer price sensitivity limited 
firms’ ability to fully pass through higher costs.” This situation 
was expected to persist.  
Local Data
In Australia, the October CPI came in a touch stronger than 
expected on both a headline basis (0.5%mth/4.9%yr) and a 
trimmed mean basis (0.5%mth/3.8%yr), although this will 
likely mark the peak in inflation (see here). This data had 
no implications for our RBA view (see here). Meanwhile, 
construction activity surprised to the upside in Q3, up 0.9%, 
driven by dwelling construction and non-res building (see here).

### Page 2

The 10yr yield held in the mid-to-low 4.9% area, with, if anything, a
tendency to test lower in yield, which is the reverse of what would
be expected from a resumed risk-on day. The movement in the
price of oil had a more signiﬁcant correlation eﬀect, with Brent
managing to dip below $90/bbl. This is clearly helpful for headline
inﬂation rates down the line, and will show up in the December
inﬂation readings (reﬂecting November).
That said, the October personal consumption expenditure (PCE)

inﬂation reading is due on Wednesday, and it's not going to be
pretty. The good news is the market should already be anticipating
an acceleration in core PCE inﬂation to 3.2% year-on-year (YoY) (up
from 3.1% YoY). The bad news is this is still a high number, and it
continues to head in the wrong direction. Also, the fall in energy
prices will have a far less direct eﬀect on core PCE. Ultimately, it will,
of course, have some eﬀect, as, for example, airline fares can ease.

That apart, the bond market remains in a troubling environment
over the coming months. We suspect that, while the risk-on mode
seen on Monday may not be repeated immediately, the recent sell-
oﬀs in the AI space are, in fact, healthy over the medium term. They
can ultimately result in risk coming back on by the turn of the year.
On top of that, ﬁscal narratives stretching from Germany (defence)
to France (already bloated) to the US (untamed) are all adding to a

sense of heightened risks for core bonds. The big swing factor could
be inﬂation. But it's just too early to declare victory on that front
yet. So, we maintain a cautious bond view for the next few months.

Markets will likely remain focused on the bounce back in AI
stocks. Eurozone data includes Spanish PPIs for October,
which, given the focus on second-round eﬀects, will be
watched. Other releases include Germany's Ifo survey results.

in November. From the US we have the Conference Board

Document 439: Who finances private credit in the United States_ What the Federal Reserve’s data reveal (2026-10-28)

Source PDF: all report pdf/Who finances private credit in the United States_ What the Federal Reserve’s data reveal (2026-10-28).pdf
Report source: BNP Paribas
Date: 2026-10-28
Pages: 3
Status: ok

### Page 1

28 October 2026
The bank
for a changing
world
WHO FINANCES PRIVATE CREDIT IN THE UNITED STATES? WHAT THE FEDERAL RESERVE’S DATA REVEAL
Céline Choulet
SOURCE: FEDERAL RESERVE (ENHANCED FINANCIAL ACCOUNTS), BNP PARIBAS
The private credit market raises many questions, and its lack of transparency is not helping. Official public data remain scarce. The available
estimates come mainly from private sources, whose methodologies vary widely. The Financial Accounts of the Federal Reserve (Fed) now shed

new light on the matter. They reveal that private credit finances 7.1% of US corporate debt through households (31% of funds’ total financing),
insurers and pension funds (20%), and banks (19%).
Private credit can take several forms. The most common is loans granted by closed-end funds1 to small and medium-sized, unlisted and highly
indebted companies. These loans are generally variable-rate, with flexible terms based on cash flow rather than a measure of profitability. While

this asset class has inherent vulnerabilities2 (Bank of France, 2025), it is also particularly well-suited to financing innovative and long-term
projects (such as digital3, infrastructure, renewable energy and healthcare), especially when combined with an equity stake in the company. Its
slower growth in Europe likely explains, in part, the region’s lag in innovation financing.
Private credit finances 7.1% of US corporate debt…
Private credit has grown rapidly in the United States over the past decade and has established itself as an alternative to bank loans for

a category of borrowers. It now accounts for 17.3% of the total outstanding loans to American non-financial corporations (NFCs)4 and 7.1%
1 Strictly speaking, a closed-end fund has a fixed term during which investors cannot withdraw their invested capital. However, the sector has diversified since 2020. The Fed distinguishes
between 1) strictly closed-end private funds (private debt funds), 2) business development companies, which may have an indefinite lifespan and allow for occasional redemptions of

shares, and 3) interval and tender offer funds, which offer the possibility of scheduled redemptions.
2 Notably, high credit risk, high leverage, difficulty in valuing loans, opaque credit ratings, occasionally complex credit structures, high concentration within the sector and increasing
liquidity risk.
3 In 2025, nearly half of the direct loans made by private credit funds went to the tech sector (software, data centres and artificial intelligence) (Abbassi et al., 2026).

4 No comparable data exist for the Eurozone. According to France Invest (2026) data, private credit issued by French funds accounted for 4% of annual bank credit flows to NFCs in 2025.
Who finances US private credit funds?

### Page 2

2
The bank for
a changing
world
Chart of the Week / 28 October 2026 economic-research.bnpparibas.com
of their debt. Approximately one-third (35%) is managed by strictly closed-end funds, with another third (36%) by semi-open-end funds
that allow occasional early redemptions. The remaining third is held on the balance sheets of non-resident private debt funds (25%) and
securitisation vehicles (4%).
… through banks, insurers and pension funds, and households

In yet another example of the lengthening of the credit intermediation chain in the United States (following that of the mortgage market),
banks are among the main creditors of private credit funds (65% of their debt and 19% of their total financing). The low risk weighting
of loans granted by banks to these vehicles, compared to that of direct bank loans to non-financial corporations, is undoubtedly a factor
(Chernenko et al., 2024). The holdings of insurers and pension funds – consisting mainly of shares in strictly closed-end funds – make

them among the leading investors in private debt funds (30% of issued shares and 20% of total financing). This asset class offers them the
opportunity to diversify their portfolios and attractive returns. The rise of semi-open-end funds has expanded the investor base to include
retail investors (31% of total financing), albeit at the cost of increased liquidity risk, as evidenced by the significant wave of redemptions in
late 2025 and early 20265. American households directly hold 70% of the equity issued by semi-open funds and 22% of the shares issued

by strictly closed funds6.
Abbassi, P., Aldasoro, I., and Doerr, S. (2026), Financing the digital economy: the role of private credit, BIS Quarterly Review, September
2026
Banque de France (2025), The reshaping of the credit landscape in light of the development of private debt and Its implications for
financial stability, Financial Stability Report, December 2025
Chernenko, S., Ialenti, R. and Scharfstein, D. (2024), Bank capital and the growth of private credit, Working paper

France Invest (2026), Private debt fund activity in France, March 2026

5 Triggered by concerns regarding private credit funds’ high exposure to the software sector and the quality of the loans granted.
6 This exposure is likely overestimated because, in the Fed’s financial accounts, the household sector includes individuals and non-profit institutions, as well as resident private
equity funds, which form partnerships with private credit funds. In order to align more closely with the European definition, households here include sole proprietors.

差擴大主要反映市場對AI經濟效益的重新評估，以
及對評級較低、資本密集度較高的發行人要求更高
的風險溢酬，並非潛在的信用風險事件或整體信用
基本面惡化，不過仍須留意目前總體經濟環境對於
信用評級較低的發行人而言更容易受到長期高利率
和成本壓力的影響，高低評等發行人的市場表現分
歧或將加大。
另一方面，回顧2026年第三季美投資級債評等變動
不多，2026年迄今的評級上調/下調比率為2.3倍；
雖然這一比率低於2022-2024年期間強勁的升評趨

勢，但高於2025年，且已是第六年大於一倍，反映
投資級債整體信用環境維持良好。

《歐洲信用債市受法國財政疑慮波及》
歐洲信用市場雖受AI相關債券波動影響較小，但因
能源價格高企，加上近期法國主權和財政風險加劇，
歐洲信用債市十月份表現也有不小波動，尤其是非
投資級債信用利差一度擴大逾60個基點。歐洲銀行
債是各產業信用債主權風險曝險較高的類別，因歐
洲銀行多數不及美國銀行營運遍及全球且業務多元，
此外也持有不少主權債資產，十月份市場表現也反
映此一狀況，主權債的波動更加重了歐洲銀行債的
跌幅，法國銀行債利差擴大幅度最為明顯，所幸法
國主要銀行包括法國巴黎銀行以及興業銀行3Q26獲
利狀況良好，即使經營環境惡化，從不良貸款率與

信用風險成本等資產品質指標來看法國巴黎銀行持
平前季、法國興業銀行僅微幅上升。
目前市場關注的焦點仍是法國財政整頓的路徑，法
國目前的債務負擔約佔GDP的118%至119%，高盛
預計，如果沒有更有效的財政調整，到2030年初負
債比率將進一步上升至接近125%，如要穩定債務
佔GDP的比重，法國需要實現約佔GDP 1%的財政
盈餘，但目前的財政赤字約佔2.5% GDP。法國政
府當局可能在年底前通過一項撙節的預算案，若沒
通過法國主權債與信用債可能進一步惡化，但即使
通過法國主權債利率要大幅回落的機會仍不大，可
能要到2027年法國大選後主權債才有機會趨於穩定，
屆時法國銀行債或有布局機會。
歐洲可供吸收損失銀行債信用利差(基點)
資料來源：高盛，2026/10，中國信託商業銀行整理，2026/10

重點摘要
• 高利率持續下信用基本面都
已 出 現分 化，如CCC等級的
償債能力指標顯著疲弱
• 衡量整體債信風險仍是歐洲
較美國為高
《非投資級債美國仍優於歐洲》
儘管目前不論是美國還是歐洲的非投資級債在高利
率持續下信用基本面都已出現分化，如CCC等級的
償債能力指標顯著疲弱，但衡量整體債信風險仍是
歐洲較美國為高；原因包括歐洲的債務到期牆較美
國明顯，且美國非投資級債2028年前到期的債券分
佈在相對廣泛的行業，而當前的賤售債券主要集中
在媒體、原物料與電信、非必需消費等特定領域，
上述較少重疊降低落入賤售債券利差區間的發行人
同時面臨債務到期後償債或再融資令違約風險增加
的可能。另一方面，歐洲非投資級債的到期牆更為
陡峭，且市場利率與2028年前到期債券的票面利率

差距大於美非投資級債，反映再融資壓力更鉅，尤
其是歐洲CCC等級。
分析歐洲非投資級債各產業債務到期狀況可發現
2028年前到期債務產業相對集中，且易受景氣影響
的非必需消費在到期債務與賤售債券都佔有一定比
例，另外槓桿率本就較高的電信業佔2028年前到期
債務9%，融資成本大幅上漲恐令這些發行人債信體
質進一步惡化。
資料來源：高盛，2026/10，中國信託商業銀行整理，2026/10
資料來源：高盛，2026/10，中國信託商業銀行整理，2026/10

《通訊服務龍頭短期營運雜音較多》
臉書 FY26Q3 財報營收、獲利皆優於預期。管理層
對 AI 投資變現的論述較上季更具體。AI 對廣告價
量的優化仍提供營收成長上行空間，但 Muse 規模
擴張與變現路徑短期變數仍多。谷歌同季財報反映
谷歌業務雙線推展的營運壓力。谷歌雲年增雖令人
驚豔，但搜尋業務營收出於預期的減速引發對做為
現金牛的搜尋業務競爭力的擔憂。隨著 AI Mode 相
關廣告業務全球推展、TPU 系統銷售爬坡與第三方
算力橋接陸續開始，谷歌短期雜音漸增。本行認為
兩龍頭短期上行動能較有限。但另一方面，本行認

為估值已反映大部分的逆風，與歷史估值區間對比
向下修正空間有限。因此，看法仍維持中立。

Meta Muse 的投資變現較上
季更具體，但仍待相關廣告變
現實例與規模展開。

的業務成長與獲利能力動態。

《臉書財報優於預期，谷歌財報較為錯綜…》
臉書 (META) FY26Q3 財報營收略優於預期、調整後
EPS 優於預期。但積極的資本支出擴張對獲利能力
的影響仍顯著。管理層指引顯示營收成長持續降溫。
自由現金流轉負，但資本支出低於市場預期。管理

層對 AI 投資變現的論述更聚焦在 Muse 為起點的規
模擴張帶動的商家交易與價值鏈環節滲透，較上季
更具體。在資本支出預期仍將持續，以在 FY2026-
27 最大化算力產能下，單位模型運算成本的下行曲
線將決定規模擴張的速度，值得持續留意模型迭代
與特化背後的成本下行發展，與商家接入臉書生態
系的速度。
谷歌 (GOOGL) 26Q3 財報反映谷歌業務雙線推展的
營運壓力。總營收雖約略符合共識預期，但營收組
成與預期有顯著差異。谷歌雲年增顯著高於預期，
但搜尋業務營收明顯減速且低於預期引發市場擔憂
搜尋業務競爭力。每股盈餘顯著低於預期，雖然 6-
7 成係因 SpaceX 持股的未實現評價損失，但因 AI
投資、TPU 系統銷售與搜尋成長減速導致利潤率也
大出市場預期之外。隨著 AI Mode 相關廣告業務全
球推展、TPU 系統銷售爬坡與第三方算力橋接陸續
開始，市場將更加聚焦檢視雙線的業務成長與獲利
能力動態。

### Page 1

NISAとiDeCoの税制優遇

資産運用調査部　シニア研究員　重原 正明（しげはら まさあき）
　国民の資産形成を促進する仕組みの一つに、税制優遇
がある。NISA（少額投資非課税制度）の投資限度額や
iDeCo（個人型確定拠出年金）の拠出枠をどう使おうかと
考えている人も多いであろう。だが、同じ税制優遇でも、
例えばNISAとiDeCoとではその中身が違うことは、あま
り意識されていないようである。以下、NISAとiDeCoに
代表される２つの税制優遇制度のパターンについて解説
する。
　NISAの場合は、投資する金額に関して税額控除や所得
控除といった税優遇は与えられない。所得税などを差し引
かれた後の所得をもとに投資を行う。ただしその投資に
よって得られた収益は非課税となり、受け取るときは元本
と投資収益分を合わせた受取額全体が非課税となる。
　最初に課税（Taxed：T）され、運用収益は課税免除
（Exempt：E）、受取時も非課税（E）ということで、このよう
な税優遇制度はTEEと表されることがある。制度としては
税優遇となる投資収益のみを把握するだけで済むので、
実施は比較的容易と考えられる。
　TEEの制度としては、NISAの他に、障がい者などが利
用できるマル優（預貯金）、特別マル優（国債・地方債）の
制度がある。
　これに対してiDeCoの場合は、拠出する金額（掛金）の
分については所得税や住民税がかからない。掛金が税法
上の「小規模企業共済等掛金控除」という控除項目とな
り、税法上の所得金額から差し引かれるからである。また、
掛金から生じた投資収益にも、収益が生じた時点では税
金がかからない。
税制優遇といっても2種類ある
NISAは受取時非課税（TEE）
iDeCoは拠出時非課税（EET）
　一方、iDeCoの資産を引き出す（受け取る）際には、受取
金額全体が課税の対象となる。ただし60歳以上になって
から、年金で受け取ったり、一時金で受け取ったりする場
合には、それぞれ公的年金等控除や退職所得控除の対象
となり、税の優遇が受けられる。
　iDeCoは拠出時非課税（E）、運用益非課税（E）、受取時
課税（T）ということでEETと表されることがある。EETの制
度としては、他に厚生年金や国民年金といった公的年金制
度、企業年金（DBおよびDC）、国民年金基金などがある。
割と制度として確立し、その分制約が強いものが多いよう
に思われる。
　TEEの制度の場合、実際には引き出す時にならないと
税優遇の恩恵を受けることができない。EETであれば投
資をする時点で税優遇のメリットを受けることができる。
また、EETでは課税される時期が一般的に所得の減る高
齢期になるため、税率が低くなる可能性もある。
　一方、EETの制度の場合、引出時には元本だけでなく運
用益分についても税金の対象となる。従って、運用益の課
税を単に繰り延べるだけとなる可能性もある（ただし先に
述べたような控除制度での優遇がある場合もある）。TEE
の場合には運用益には税金は全くかからない。
　他の制度との関係もあり、NISAのようなTEEとiDeCo
のようなEETと、どちらが有利かは一概には判断できな
い。大切なのは、税制優遇というだけで飛びつくのではな
く、その中身を知って活用することではないだろうか。税
制優遇制度を正しく活用することで、読者のウェルビーイ
ングが高まることを願う。
（注 ： 本稿は制度の解説を行ったものであり、税務会計上のアドバイスを
行うものではありません）
TEEとEETのメリット・デメリット
各制度の内容を知ったうえで税制優遇の活用を
9 第一ライフ研レポート 2026.12

### Page 1

2026 年 10 月 20 日

調査レポート

コモディティ・レポート（2026 年 7～9 月）

調査部 主任研究員 芥田 知至 
1. コモディティ市況全般：原油が上昇傾向、銅は最高値更新、金は上値重い 
ドル建て国際商品市況全般の動向を示す FTSE・コアコモディティ CRB 指数は、2023 年 5 月 31 日に 2022
年 1 月以来の安値をつけた後、2026 年 3～9 月には大幅に上昇して 9 月 10 日に 2008 年 7 月以来の高値を
つけた。その後、10 月にかけてやや下落した。コモディティ相場は、中東情勢や米金融政策の行方を巡って、引
き続き、不安定な推移が見こまれる。

2. エネルギー市況：足元はブレントが一時 110 ドル、WTI が 100 ドル前後 
国際指標のブレント原油は、2026 年 4 月 30 日に 126.41 ドルと 2022 年 3 月以来の高値をつけた。足元は
110 ドル前後で不安定な動きとなっている。米国とイランは戦闘終結やホルムズ海峡再開に向けた動きは停滞し
たままであり、緊迫する中東情勢を受けて原油は高止まりが見こまれる。

3. ベースメタル市況：銅は 9 月上旬に 14,875 ドルの史上最高値 
銅相場は、2026 年 9 月 10 日に 14,875 ドルと史上最高値を記録し、足元は 14,000 ドル台半ばを中心に推
移している。銅は、原油高や景気悪化の影響が懸念される一方で、AI・エネルギー転換・防衛の関連分野を中
心に需要が伸びるとの見方が根強く、高値圏での一進一退が見こまれる。

4. 貴金属市況：金は 8 月中旬に 4,700 ドル近くまで持ち直すも足元は 4,100 ドル前後 
金は、2026 年 1 月 29 日に 5,594.82 ドルと史上最高値を記録した後、6 月 30 日には 3,942.99 ドルまで下
落し、足元は 4,100 ドル前後を中心に推移している。米利上げが続くとの観測が有力な中、中東情勢も安定化に
はまだ遠く、原油高が意識されて、金は買われにくい状況が続きやすいとみられる。

1. コモディティ市況全般の概況：原油が上昇傾向、銅は最高値更新、金は上値重い 
ドル建て国際商品市況全般の動向を示す FTSE・コアコモディティ CRB 指数（終値ベース）は、2022 年 6 月
9 日に 2011 年 9 月以来の高値をつけた後、2023 年 5 月 31 日には 2022 年 1 月以来の安値をつけた。2026
年 3～9 月に大幅に上昇し、9 月 10 日に 2008 年 7 月以来の高値をつけた。10 月にかけてやや下落した。 
マクロ経済環境をみると、フーシ派とサウジアラビアによる攻撃の応酬が激しさを増すなど中東の混乱に広がり
がみられ、米国とイランも戦闘終結やホルムズ海峡再開へ向けた動きは停滞したままである。原油は緊迫する中
東情勢を受けて上昇傾向で推移した。金は、原油高などを受けた根強い米利上げ観測を背景に上値が重い状
況が続いた。銅は、原油高や景気悪化が懸念される一方で、AI・エネルギー転換・防衛の関連分野を中心に需
要が伸びるとの見方が根強く、史上最高値圏で一進一退となった。中東情勢や米金融政策を巡って、コモディテ
ィ相場は不安定な推移が続くと見こまれる。
